### Microbiota boost immunotherapy? A meta-analysis dives into fecal microbiota transplantation and immune checkpoint inhibitors

- Source handle: `P0212`
- Paper ID: `5af3aee4ca5f3778325d44c8432c8b71b95ca531`
- DOI: 10.1186/s12916-025-04183-y

  - **Available material depth:** fulltext

#### Paper understanding from a
    - **Approach:** 采用PRISMA指南指导的系统综述方法。检索策略覆盖PubMed、ClinicalTrials.gov及主要肿瘤学会议摘要。纳入标准限定为接受ICI联合FMT治疗的实体瘤患者，排除仅关注免疫微环境变化或无具体数据的回顾性研究。使用Cochrane ROB 2.0和ROBINS-I工具评估偏倚风险。统计分析使用Stata 18和R语言，以ORR为主要终点，CR、PR、PFS、OS及不良事件为次要终点。对于生存数据，因多数研究未报告风险比（HR），采用加权平均中位数进行初步评估。亚组分析基于ICI策略、供体来源、给药途径和肿瘤类型进行。
##### Contribution and limits
    - **1.**
      - **Contribution:** 提供了FMT联合ICI治疗实体瘤的汇总疗效数据（ORR 43%），并量化了不同ICI策略（特别是双药联合）对疗效的显著影响。
      - **边界与限制:** 所给材料未交代具体的微生物组测序深度、特定菌株定植率的长期追踪数据，以及FMT对ICI耐药性的逆转机制的直接实验证据。
    - **2.**
      - **Contribution:** 明确了FMT联合治疗的安全性特征，指出其不显著增加严重不良事件风险，甚至可能在某些情况下降低irAEs。
      - **边界与限制:** 安全性数据主要来自短期随访，缺乏长期安全性监测，无法评估迟发性不良反应或微生物组改变的持久性。
    - **3.**
      - **Contribution:** 识别了关键的方法学异质性来源，如供体选择（ICI反应者vs健康人）和给药途径对结果的潜在影响。
      - **边界与限制:** 纳入研究多为单臂或小样本RCT，缺乏大规模多中心对照，导致效应估计可能存在选择偏倚，且无法确立因果关系。
##### Key findings
    - **1.**
      - **适用条件:** 所有纳入的10项研究（n=164），混合ICI方案
      - **研究发现:** 总体客观缓解率（ORR）为43% (95% CI: 0.35–0.51)，存在中度异质性 (I² = 59%)。
    - **2.**
      - **适用条件:** 亚组：抗PD-1联合抗CTLA-4双药治疗 vs 抗PD-1单药
      - **研究发现:** 双药治疗ORR显著更高（60% vs 37%, P=0.01），且双药组内异质性低 (I² = 0)。
    - **3.**
      - **适用条件:** 亚组：供体来源为ICI反应者 vs 健康人或商业制剂
      - **研究发现:** ICI反应者供体的ORR较高（46%），但组间差异无统计学意义；该亚组内存在显著异质性 (I² = 66%)。
    - **4.**
      - **适用条件:** 亚组：口服粪菌胶囊 vs 内镜移植
      - **研究发现:** 口服胶囊ORR较高（49% vs 34%），但组间差异无统计学意义；该亚组内存在显著异质性 (I² = 68%)。
    - **5.**
      - **适用条件:** 安全性评估：所有研究
      - **研究发现:** 1-2级不良事件发生率42% (95% CI: 0.32–0.52)，3-4级为37% (95% CI: 0.28–0.46)。双药治疗组的3-4级AE发生率高于单药组。
    - **6.**
      - **适用条件:** 生存分析：部分研究提供的中位数据
      - **研究发现:** 加权平均中位无进展生存期（mPFS）为8.32个月，中位总生存期（mOS）为12.67个月。
    - **Paper kind:** review
    - **Problem or question:** 解决FMT在增强ICI疗效方面的临床证据碎片化问题，明确其有效性、安全性以及不同治疗策略（如ICI组合、供体来源、给药途径）对疗效的影响，为未来临床试验设计提供依据。
    - **Research scope:** 针对晚期或难治性实体瘤患者，评估粪便微生物群移植（FMT）联合免疫检查点抑制剂（ICI）治疗的临床疗效与安全性。纳入的研究包括随机对照试验（RCT）、单臂干预研究和前瞻性队列研究，涵盖黑色素瘤、肾细胞癌（RCC）、微卫星稳定型结直肠癌（MSS-CRC）等多种肿瘤类型。
    - **Work summary:** 这是一篇系统综述与荟萃分析，旨在综合现有证据以评估FMT作为增强ICI疗效辅助策略的临床价值。研究通过系统检索PubMed及临床试验注册库，筛选出10项涉及164名患者的研究进行定量合成。主要发现显示，FMT联合ICI的总体客观缓解率（ORR）为43%，其中抗PD-1联合抗CTLA-4双药治疗的ORR显著高于单药治疗（60% vs 37%）。安全性方面，1-2级不良事件发生率为42%，3-4级为37%。尽管结果呈现初步积极信号，但作者指出由于纳入研究多为早期小样本试验且缺乏长期随访数据，结论尚需大规模RCT进一步验证。

#### Review planning account from b
##### Broader review uses
    - **1.**
      - **Connection to review:** 提供FMT在ICI治疗中的安全性背景，特别是关于免疫相关不良事件（irAEs）的发生率。
      - **Material:** 1-2级AE发生率42%，3-4级为37%。双药治疗组3-4级AE更高。FMT可能具有减轻某些irAEs（如结肠炎）的潜力。
      - **本节作用:** 用于评估微生物干预的安全边界，讨论其在联合治疗中的风险收益比。
    - **2.**
      - **Connection to review:** 提供当前FMT临床研究的局限性，如缺乏长期随访、标准化协议缺失等。
      - **Material:** 纳入研究多为早期小样本试验，缺乏大规模RCT，长期疗效和安全性未知。
      - **本节作用:** 用于指出领域内的知识空白，强调未来研究需要标准化的微生物组管理和长期监测。
##### Facet contributions
    - **1.**
      - **Boundaries:** 仅反映临床终点（ORR/PFS/OS），未直接测量微生物群的具体组成变化与疗效之间的因果链条。异质性较高，不能代表所有患者群体。
      - **Contribution:** 提供了FMT联合ICI在实体瘤中的汇总临床疗效数据（ORR 43%），证实了微生物干预对ICI疗效的正向调节作用。特别指出抗PD-1/CTLA-4双药联合时疗效显著提升（ORR 60%），提示微生物群可能增强双重阻断的协同效应。
      - **Facet id:** F1
      - **Possible uses:** 用于回答“肠道微生物群是否以及如何调节ICI疗效”的问题，提供临床层面的有效性证据。可用于支持“微生物群是ICI疗效的关键决定因素”这一论点，并展示不同治疗策略下的疗效差异。
    - **2.**
      - **Boundaries:** 这些是作者基于已有文献提出的假设，并非本荟萃分析直接验证的机制。缺乏直接的分子生物学证据（如单细胞测序、代谢组学数据）来证实这些机制在FMT患者中的实际运作路径。
      - **Contribution:** 提出了FMT增强ICI疗效的潜在机制假说：(1) 增加有益菌（如Akkermansia muciniphila, Bifidobacterium）；(2) 提高肠道微生物α多样性；(3) 调节肿瘤微环境（TME），促进效应T细胞浸润并抑制调节性T细胞。
      - **Facet id:** F2
      - **Possible uses:** 用于回答“介导该效应的机制是什么”的问题，提供基于文献的理论解释框架。可作为后续深入探讨具体菌株功能或代谢产物作用的背景知识。
    - **3.**
      - **Boundaries:** 亚组分析样本量小，异质性大，结果仅为趋势性观察，不能作为确定性的指导原则。
      - **Contribution:** 揭示了供体来源（ICI反应者 vs 健康人）和给药途径（口服胶囊 vs 内镜）对疗效的潜在影响趋势，尽管统计上不显著，但提示了个性化FMT策略的重要性。
      - **Facet id:** F1
      - **Possible uses:** 用于讨论“哪些因素可能影响微生物群调节ICI疗效的效果”，为制定个性化治疗方案提供依据。
    - **Planning summary:** 该论文为综述“肠道微生物群如何调节实体瘤中ICI疗效”提供了关键的临床关联证据。它证实了通过FMT重塑微生物群确实能提升ICI的客观缓解率，特别是在双药联合背景下。同时，它指出了当前临床转化的瓶颈：异质性高、缺乏标准化协议、以及机制不明。对于机制部分，它提供了理论假设框架（如增加有益菌、调节TME），但未提供直接的分子机制验证。
##### Scope interpretation cautions
    - 该研究为荟萃分析，其结论依赖于纳入的10项研究的质量和设计。大部分研究为单臂或小样本RCT，存在较高的选择偏倚风险，因此其报告的疗效提升不能完全归因于FMT，也可能受患者选择等因素影响。
    - 亚组分析（如供体来源、给药途径）的结果虽显示趋势，但因样本量小和异质性高，不具备统计显著性，解读时应谨慎，避免过度推断。
    - 机制部分仅为作者基于文献的推测，非本研究直接验证，引用时需明确区分观察性关联与因果机制。

#### Other supplement material
  - **1.**
      - **Auxiliary material:** The study provides detailed safety data (42% grade 1–2 adverse events, 37% grade 3–4 adverse events) and mechanistic hypotheses (e.g., increasing beneficial bacteria like Akkermansia muciniphila, modulating tumor microenvironment) which, while not validating the Phase III gap, offer context on why such trials are being pursued. It also notes heterogeneity in FMT protocols (donor sources, administration routes) as a barrier that large-scale trials need to standardize.
      - **Gap id:** phase_iii_rct_evidence
      - **Judgment path:** F:\OptoMind-Review-2\outputs\body_full_staged_acceptance_20261004_40cny\planning\level2\external\N48436b3aa73aea86f35f-f45b87be8e30256e\round_1\supplements\phase_iii_rct_evidence\attempt-c7j_i2fn\source_units\SU01_search_candidate_d1c4b32e8c\FULFILLMENT_JUDGMENT.json
      - **Material ready:** True
      - **Material usefulness:** supporting
      - **Outline action:** Use this meta-analysis to substantiate the claim that current evidence is limited to early-phase (I/II) and small-scale RCTs. Cite the authors' conclusion regarding the necessity of large-scale Phase III trials to justify the gap. Do not present the 3 included RCTs as fulfilling the 'large-scale Phase III' criterion, as the study itself classifies them as limited and calls for further validation.
      - **Outline action recovered by policy:** False
      - **Remaining gap:** The specific evidence requested—completed large-scale randomized controlled Phase III clinical trials confirming efficacy—is not present in the literature covered by this study. The 164 patients across 10 studies do not constitute a large-scale Phase III dataset, and the authors explicitly state the need for such trials to validate efficacy and safety.
      - **Remaining gap recovered by policy:** False
      - **Status:** unmet
      - **Useful material:** This study is a systematic review and meta-analysis (search up to September 30, 2024) that included 10 studies involving 164 patients with solid tumors treated with FMT combined with ICIs. The authors report that the included studies comprised 5 phase I trials, 4 phase II–III trials, and 3 randomized controlled trials (RCTs), but note that most were early-phase clinical trials with small sample sizes. The pooled objective response rate (ORR) was 43%, with subgroup analysis showing higher ORR (60%) for anti-PD-1 combined with anti-CTLA-4 therapy compared to monotherapy (37%). Crucially, the authors conclude in both the Abstract and Discussion that 'larger-scale randomized controlled trials with long-term follow-up are required to confirm and optimize treatment protocols' and highlight the 'absence of randomized controlled trials' (referring to large-scale/definitive ones) as a limitation. This material directly addresses the gap question by confirming that while preliminary RCT data exists, completed large-scale Phase III evidence is currently lacking and identified as a necessary future step.

### Improved survival in advanced melanoma patients treated with fecal microbiota transplantation using healthy donor stool in combination with anti-PD1: final results of the MIMic phase 1 trial

- Source handle: `P0577`
- Paper ID: `20c143d5373eab0bfea2067cf66b0e260dfeb1ef`
- DOI: 10.1136/jitc-2025-012659

  - **Available material depth:** fulltext

#### Paper understanding from a
    - **Approach:** Single-arm, multicenter phase I clinical trial. Patients received bowel preparation, followed by oral administration of capsules containing stool from one of three healthy male donors. Anti-PD1 therapy (nivolumab or pembrolizumab) started at least 7 days post-FMT. Outcomes were assessed using RECIST 1.1 criteria. Survival analysis used Kaplan-Meier methods. Metagenomic sequencing was performed on stool samples to analyze taxonomic composition (MetaPhlAn) and functional gene families (HUMAnN). Post hoc subgroup analyses compared responders vs. non-responders and patients with vs. without FMT-specific toxicity (FT) based on microbial and functional profiles.
##### Contribution and limits
    - **1.**
      - **Contribution:** Provides long-term survival data (median OS 52.8 months) for a small cohort of advanced melanoma patients treated with oral FMT plus anti-PD1, demonstrating high safety and promising efficacy signals compared to historical controls.
      - **边界与限制:** Small sample size (N=20), single-arm design without a control group, and lack of statistical power for definitive conclusions. Results are hypothesis-generating. Patient selection bias (excluded those appropriate for combination therapy).
    - **2.**
      - **Contribution:** Identifies potential biomarkers for response, linking FMT-specific mild toxicity and long-term survival to specific microbial taxa (e.g., SCFA producers) and functional metabolic pathways (glycan modification, SCFA biosynthesis).
      - **边界与限制:** Associations are derived from post hoc analyses. Causality between specific microbes/enzymes and clinical benefit is not established. The 'donor effect' could not be clearly disentangled due to the small cohort and mixed donor usage.
##### Key findings
    - **1.**
      - **适用条件:** Entire cohort (N=20), median follow-up 52.8 months
      - **研究发现:** Median PFS was 29.6 months; median OS was 52.8 months. ORR was 65% (4 complete responses). Safety profile showed no increase in severe immune-related adverse events (irAEs); only grade 3 toxicities occurred.
    - **2.**
      - **适用条件:** Responders (R, N=13) vs. Non-responders (NR, N=7)
      - **研究发现:** R patients had significantly longer mPFS (52.8 vs. 5.2 months, p=0.0016) and longer mOS (52.8 vs. 17.6 months, p=0.0628). R patients sustained donor-derived microbial similarity over time.
    - **3.**
      - **适用条件:** Patients with FMT-specific toxicity (FT, N=8) vs. Without FT (NFT, N=12)
      - **研究发现:** All 8 FT patients were responders. FT patients had significantly improved mPFS (52.8 vs. 15.9 months, p=0.0364) and trend towards improved mOS (53.9 vs. 24.6 months, p=0.0610). FT was associated with enrichment of SCFA-producing taxa and glycan-modifying enzymes.
    - **4.**
      - **适用条件:** Long-term survivors (LTS) vs. Short-term survivors (STS)
      - **研究发现:** LTS were enriched for commensal bacteria (Alistipes, Faecalibacterium) and enzymes involved in amino acid/SCFA biosynthesis. STS were enriched for stress-associated oxidative metabolism pathways and Hungatella hathewayi.
    - **Paper kind:** empirical
    - **Problem or question:** To evaluate whether modifying the gut microbiome via oral FMT from healthy donors can improve the safety, response rate, and survival outcomes of anti-PD1 therapy in patients with advanced melanoma who are naive to advanced-stage treatment, potentially overcoming primary resistance to immune checkpoint inhibitors (ICIs).
    - **Research scope:** Advanced cutaneous melanoma patients (N=20), treatment-naïve for advanced disease, receiving oral fecal microbiota transplantation (FMT) from healthy donors followed by single-agent anti-PD1 therapy. Multicenter phase I trial conducted in Canada.
    - **Work summary:** This study reports the final survival outcomes of the MIMic phase I trial, evaluating the safety and efficacy of combining oral FMT with healthy donor stool and anti-PD1 immunotherapy as first-line treatment for advanced melanoma. The trial enrolled 20 patients who received a single dose of FMT followed by nivolumab or pembrolizumab. The primary endpoint was safety, while secondary endpoints included objective response rate (ORR), progression-free survival (PFS), and overall survival (OS). The study found that the combination was safe, with an ORR of 65%. Median PFS was 29.6 months and median OS was 52.8 months, figures reported as higher than historical randomized controlled trials (RCTs) and real-world data. Post hoc analyses linked clinical responses to specific microbial engraftment patterns and functional metabolic pathways, suggesting that successful microbiome modulation may enhance ICI efficacy.

#### Review planning account from b
##### Broader review uses
    - **1.**
      - **Connection to review:** Helps categorize different microbiome intervention modalities in the review.
      - **Material:** Comparison of oral capsule-based FMT (this study) vs. invasive colonoscopic FMT (referenced in intro). This study supports the feasibility and safety of oral delivery for systemic immune modulation.
      - **本节作用:** Contextual background on FMT delivery methods
    - **2.**
      - **Connection to review:** Addresses the safety facet of microbiome interventions, countering concerns about increased autoimmune toxicity when combining FMT with ICIs.
      - **Material:** No increase in severe irAEs; FMT-specific toxicities were mild (Grade 1-2 GI).
      - **本节作用:** Safety profile of microbiome manipulation
    - **3.**
      - **Connection to review:** Useful for discussing the quality of evidence in the field and the need for larger, randomized Phase II/III trials (like the mentioned ME.17 trial).
      - **Material:** Acknowledges small sample size, lack of control arm, and hypothesis-generating nature of results.
      - **本节作用:** Limitations of current evidence
##### Facet contributions
    - **1.**
      - **Boundaries:** Results are from a single-arm, small-sample Phase I trial. Cannot establish causal superiority over standard care due to lack of concurrent control arm. Patient population was restricted to those unsuitable for combination immunotherapy.
      - **Contribution:** Reports clinical efficacy outcomes (ORR 65%, mPFS 29.6 mo, mOS 52.8 mo) for FMT+anti-PD1 in advanced melanoma, showing improvements over historical RCT benchmarks for anti-PD1 alone.
      - **Facet id:** F1
      - **Possible uses:** Can be used to illustrate the potential magnitude of benefit from microbiome manipulation in solid tumors, specifically melanoma. Provides baseline survival metrics for comparison with other microbiome interventions.
    - **2.**
      - **Boundaries:** Mechanisms are inferred from correlative post hoc analyses. The role of FMT-specific toxicity as a proxy for engraftment success is observational. Functional profiling relies on metagenomic prediction, which may not reflect actual protein activity or host interaction.
      - **Contribution:** Links clinical response to specific microbial features: Responders and long-term survivors show enrichment of SCFA-producing taxa (Blautia, Roseburia, Faecalibacterium) and enzymes for glycan modification/amino acid biosynthesis. Non-responders/short-term survivors show enrichment of oxidative/stress pathways and Hungatella hathewayi.
      - **Facet id:** F2
      - **Possible uses:** Provides mechanistic insights into how microbiome composition might mediate ICI efficacy. Suggests that successful engraftment of metabolically fit, SCFA-producing communities enhances immune response. Identifies functional metabolic signatures (vs. just taxonomy) associated with outcome.
    - **Planning summary:** This paper provides empirical evidence from a Phase I trial that oral FMT from healthy donors combined with anti-PD1 is safe and associated with improved survival in advanced melanoma. It offers specific mechanistic hypotheses linking clinical response to microbial engraftment success, characterized by the enrichment of short-chain fatty acid (SCFA)-producing bacteria and specific metabolic pathways. The study highlights FMT-specific toxicity as a potential marker of successful microbial integration and immune priming.
##### Scope interpretation cautions
    - The study population is limited to advanced cutaneous melanoma patients ineligible for combination immunotherapy; results may not generalize to other solid tumors or earlier-stage disease.
    - Survival comparisons with historical RCTs are noted by authors but are confounded by patient selection biases and temporal changes in care; do not treat as definitive proof of superiority.
    - Microbial associations are correlational; causality between specific taxa/pathways and ICI response is not proven within this study design.
##### Topic handles
    - oral fecal microbiota transplantation (FMT) in solid tumors
    - anti-PD1 monotherapy in advanced melanoma
    - microbial engraftment dynamics (donor-recipient similarity)
    - FMT-specific gastrointestinal toxicity as a response biomarker
    - metabolic pathways: SCFA production, glycan degradation, oxidative stress

#### Supplement material
    - **Auxiliary material:** The paper provides mechanistic insights linking clinical response to microbial engraftment, noting that responders and long-term survivors showed enrichment of short-chain fatty acid (SCFA)-produ taxa (e.g., Blautia, Roseburia, Faecalibacterium) and glycan-modifying enzymes. It also suggests that mild FMT-specific gastrointestinal toxicity may serve as a marker for successful microbial integration and immune priming. Additionally, the text contrasts these findings with historical RCT data (e.g., KEYNOTE-006, CheckMate 067) to contextualize the magnitude of observed benefits, though it acknowledges limitations regarding patient selection bias.

    - **Useful material:** This study reports results from the MIMic trial, a multicenter, single-arm Phase I safety trial evaluating oral FMT combined with anti-PD1 therapy in 20 patients with advanced melanoma. The study found that the combination was safe and associated with an objective response rate (ORR) of 65%, median progression-free survival (mPFS) of 29.6 months, and median overall survival (mOS) of 52.8 months. These outcomes were compared favorably to historical randomized controlled trials (RCTs) of anti-PD1 monotherapy. The authors explicitly state that the sample size was small, there was no control arm, and the results are hypothesis-generating. The study identifies a subsequent randomized Phase II Canadian study (ME.17, NCT06623461) designed to evaluate the potential benefit of this intervention.

    - **Paper title:** Improved survival in advanced melanoma patients treated with fecal microbiota transplantation using healthy donor stool in combination with anti-PD1: final results of the MIMic phase 1 trial
##### Question material
    - **1.**
###### Examples
      - **1.**
      - **Attribution:** 本研究
      - **适用条件:** 研究对象为20名晚期黑色素瘤患者；供体为3名健康男性；给药方式为口服胶囊；预处理为PEG肠道准备；无抗生素使用。
      - **研究发现:** MIMic试验使用了来自三位健康男性供体的粪便进行口服FMT。所有患者均接受单一供体的粪便（每位患者对应一位供体），未混合多供体粪便。供体经过“严格的选择标准”筛选（文中引用了之前的出版物Craven et al. 2017及Routy et al. 2023中的详细标准）。FMT产品以胶囊形式给药，每剂包含35-40粒胶囊，相当于80-100克粪便。在给予FMT前一天，患者使用聚乙二醇（PEG）基肠道准备液进行预处理，且治疗期间未使用抗生素。
###### Reference ids
      - R12
      - R13
      - **Use in review:** 作为FMT干预标准化中供体筛选和制备流程的具体案例，展示如何通过严格筛选和健康供体来源来保证干预的一致性和安全性，同时指出单供体使用的局限性。
      - **Explanation:** MIMic试验在供体筛选和FMT制备方面采取了高度标准化的策略，旨在确保干预的安全性和微生物定植的可预测性。

1. **供体筛选标准**：研究采用了“严格的选择标准”（stringent selection criteria）来筛选健康供体。虽然正文未列出具体排除指标的细节，但明确引用了关于代谢综合征疾病FMT供体筛选成本的研究（Craven et al., 2017, R13）以及该团队之前发表的I期试验方案（Routy et al., 2023, R12），暗示其遵循了当时临床前或早期临床试验中较为严格的卫生学、传染病及健康状况筛查流程。这种严格筛选是作为正面教材的，因为它最大限度地降低了传播病原体或引入致病微生物的风险，确保了“健康供体粪便”这一干预物的纯净度。

2. **FMT制备与给药流程**：
   - **单一供源 vs 混合供源**：试验设计采用单一供体粪便（single healthy donor stool），每位患者仅接受来自三位指定男性供体中某一人的粪便。这种做法简化了批次管理，减少了不同供体间微生物组异质性带来的混杂因素，有利于评估特定微生物组合对免疫检查点抑制剂（ICI）的反应。然而，这也可能是一个反面教材或局限性，因为缺乏多供体混合可能无法覆盖更广泛的有益菌群多样性，且文中提到“没有明显的供体效应”，说明单个供体的效果在不同患者间可能存在差异，但未深入探讨混合供体是否会更优。
   - **制剂形式**：将粪便制成口服胶囊（35-40粒/剂，含80-100g粪便），而非传统的灌肠或鼻胃管给药。这提高了患者的依从性，减少了侵入性操作带来的感染风险，是FMT非侵入化、标准化的重要进展。
   - **预处理与辅助措施**：在FMT前一日使用PEG基肠道准备液，旨在清除原有肠道内容物，为新菌群定植腾出生态位。特别值得注意的是，研究中明确禁止使用抗生素，以避免抗生素对肠道菌群的破坏干扰FMT的效果或引起耐药性问题。这种“清洁但不杀菌”的准备策略是标准化流程中的关键控制点。

总体而言，该试验通过严格的供体准入、单一的供体来源、标准化的胶囊制剂以及特定的肠道预处理，构建了一套可重复的FMT干预流程。其成功的关键在于确保了输入菌群的健康属性和定植潜力，为后续观察微生物组变化与临床疗效的关系提供了可靠的基础。
      - **Question id:** Q01
###### Remaining points
      - 文中引用的供体筛选具体指标（如具体的病原体检测列表、BMI限制、饮食要求等）未在本文正文中详细列出，需参考R12和R13获取完整细节。
      - 单一供体与多供体混合在长期定植稳定性和临床获益上的比较数据不足，仅观察到无显著供体效应，未深入分析为何选择单一供体而非混合供体。
      - PEG肠道准备的具体配方浓度、体积及服用时间未详细说明。

### Targeting ribosomes reprograms the tumour microenvironment and augments cancer immunotherapy

- Source handle: `P0580`
- Paper ID: `c5e74d5eebf19335caebc97bdb8300261fcb0ee5`
- DOI: 10.1038/s41416-025-03109-y

  - **Available material depth:** fulltext

#### Paper understanding from a
    - **Approach:** 1. 动物实验：在C57BL/6和BALB/c小鼠中建立MC38和CT26同系移植瘤模型，给予CX-5461灌胃治疗，设置联合抗LAG3抗体治疗组。2. 单细胞测序与分析：对治疗后的小鼠肿瘤组织进行scRNA-seq，使用NMF算法构建“核糖体状态”签名以量化单个细胞的核糖体生物合成水平，识别对核糖体抑制敏感的免疫亚群。3. 公共数据挖掘：整合多个癌症类型的scRNA-seq数据集和ICB治疗队列数据，分析核糖体状态与免疫细胞分化、预后及治疗反应的关系。4. 临床验证：利用多重免疫荧光染色和空间转录组技术，在人类结直肠癌、肝细胞癌等临床样本中验证关键细胞亚群的核糖体活性及其空间分布。5. 体外验证：通过多聚核糖体分级、蛋白质折叠检测及流式细胞术确认分子机制。
##### Contribution and limits
    - **1.**
      - **Contribution:** 首次提供了核糖体靶向治疗重塑TME的单细胞图谱，揭示了非癌细胞（特别是特定T细胞和巨噬细胞亚群）的核糖体生物合成活性在维持免疫抑制微环境中的作用。
      - **边界与限制:** 主要基于小鼠同系移植瘤模型，尚未在更多类型的实体瘤或非上皮来源肿瘤中进行广泛验证；机制上主要依赖转录组关联，部分因果链条（如HSP70如何具体调控核糖体进而影响细胞命运）仍需更深入的功能实验确证。
##### Key findings
    - **1.**
      - **适用条件:** CX-5461处理的小鼠MC38/CT26肿瘤模型
      - **研究发现:** CX-5461直接激活T细胞和NK细胞，增加穿孔素和细胞因子分泌；诱导巨噬细胞从免疫抑制性（LA/Reg-TAMs）向促炎性（IFN/Inflam-TAMs）表型转变。
    - **2.**
      - **适用条件:** 基于scRNA-seq数据的核糖体状态分析
      - **研究发现:** 构建了包含73个元基因的核糖体状态签名，证实高核糖体活性与不良预后相关；鉴定出HSP70+ CD8+ T细胞（T_str）和MRC1+ TAMs具有极高的核糖体生物合成活性，且对CX-5461敏感。
    - **3.**
      - **适用条件:** ICB治疗响应患者队列
      - **研究发现:** 在响应ICB治疗的患者中，高核糖体活性的免疫抑制性T细胞亚群（如HSP70+ CD8+ T细胞和Tregs）在治疗后显著减少，表明这些细胞是维持免疫耐受的关键。
    - **4.**
      - **适用条件:** CT26模型中CX-5461与抗LAG3联合治疗
      - **研究发现:** CX-5461诱导LAG3表达，显著增敏CT26肿瘤对抗LAG3治疗的反应，导致肿瘤显著消退和更深层的抗肿瘤免疫反应，且未引起明显的体重下降或DNA损伤副作用。
    - **Paper kind:** empirical
    - **Problem or question:** 既往研究多集中于癌细胞自身的核糖体异常，而忽视了核糖体生物合成在非癌细胞（特别是免疫细胞）中的作用。目前尚不清楚阻断细胞核糖体生物合成如何影响TME中的免疫调节，以及这种干预是否能重塑免疫抑制性微环境并增强现有免疫疗法的疗效。
    - **Research scope:** 免疫健全小鼠体内的结直肠癌（MC38, CT26）同系移植瘤模型；人类多种实体瘤的单细胞转录组数据（scRNA-seq）及空间转录组数据；体内临床样本。研究对象聚焦于肿瘤微环境（TME）中的非癌细胞亚群及其核糖体生物合成活性。
    - **Work summary:** 本研究旨在探讨靶向核糖体生物合成的抑制剂CX-5461对肿瘤微环境（TME）的重编程作用及其增强免疫检查点阻断（ICB）疗效的机制。研究利用免疫健全小鼠模型进行CX-5461治疗，并结合单细胞RNA测序（scRNA-seq）、空间转录组学及临床样本验证，系统分析了TME中不同细胞类型的核糖体状态变化。研究发现，CX-5461不仅抑制癌细胞核糖体生成，还特异性地调节了TME中的免疫细胞：它促进T细胞和NK细胞的细胞毒性颗粒分泌，诱导巨噬细胞向促炎表型重编程，并显著减少具有高核糖体活性的HSP70+ CD8+ T细胞（应激反应T细胞）和调节性T细胞（Tregs）。此外，该药物诱导了LAG3等免疫检查点的表达，使原本对ICB不敏感的CT26肿瘤模型对抗LAG3疗法高度敏感，实现了显著的肿瘤消退。

#### Review planning account from b
##### Broader review uses
    - **1.**
      - **Connection to review:** 提供宿主细胞内在代谢/翻译状态影响免疫治疗的新视角，补充微生物组之外的调节机制。
      - **Material:** CX-5461诱导错折叠蛋白积累形成新抗原，增加肿瘤免疫原性；核糖体状态签名与患者生存期的负相关。
      - **本节作用:** 作为背景材料，说明肿瘤微环境的复杂性不仅限于微生物互作，还包括宿主细胞自身的翻译重编程和应激反应，这些因素共同决定了ICB的疗效。
    - **2.**
      - **Connection to review:** 提供关于LAG3在核糖体抑制背景下被诱导表达的实例。
      - **Material:** CX-5461处理后LAG3在CD8+ T细胞中高表达，且联合抗LAG3治疗显著增强疗效。
      - **本节作用:** 举例说明免疫检查点的动态变化及其作为联合治疗靶点的潜力，特别是在那些对常规PD-1/PD-L1抑制剂反应不佳的“冷”肿瘤中。
##### Facet contributions
    - **1.**
      - **Boundaries:** 该研究未涉及肠道微生物组本身，因此不能直接回答微生物组如何调制ICB疗效的问题，仅能提供宿主细胞层面的平行机制或潜在交互作用的假设基础。
      - **Contribution:** 提供了核糖体生物合成活性在TME非癌细胞中异质性分布的证据，特别是高活性与免疫抑制性表型（如Tregs, MRC1+ TAMs）的正相关性。
      - **Facet id:** F1
      - **Possible uses:** 可用于解释肠道微生物组或其他代谢因素如何通过影响宿主免疫细胞的翻译能力来间接调节ICB疗效的背景讨论；或作为对比案例，说明除了微生物代谢产物外，宿主细胞内在的翻译机器状态也是重要调节维度。
    - **2.**
      - **Boundaries:** 该机制针对的是LAG3而非PD-1/PD-L1，且依赖于特定的核糖体抑制剂。需明确区分这是宿主细胞内在机制，而非微生物介导的直接效应。
      - **Contribution:** 阐明了靶向核糖体生物合成（CX-5461）通过清除高核糖体活性的抑制性T细胞（HSP70+ CD8+ T）和Tregs，并诱导LAG3表达，从而增敏LAG3阻断疗法的机制。
      - **Facet id:** F2
      - **Possible uses:** 直接用于回答“哪些机制介导了这种效应”中的非微生物机制部分。展示了通过调节细胞内在翻译状态来改变免疫检查点表达和消除抑制性细胞的具体路径。
    - **Planning summary:** 该论文为综述提供了关于“非癌细胞核糖体功能在肿瘤免疫逃逸中的作用”及“代谢/翻译机器作为免疫治疗增敏靶点”的重要实证素材。其核心价值在于打破了仅关注癌细胞核糖体的局限，指出了TME中特定免疫亚群（HSP70+ CD8+ T, MRC1+ TAMs）的高核糖体活性是免疫抑制的关键特征，并证明了通过CX-5461靶向这一过程可逆转免疫抑制并增敏LAG3阻断疗法。这为解释ICB耐药机制及开发联合策略提供了新的视角。
##### Scope interpretation cautions
    - 该研究主要关注核糖体生物合成抑制剂CX-5461，其机制可能与其他核糖体靶向药物不同，外推时需谨慎。
    - 发现的HSP70+ CD8+ T细胞亚群及其高核糖体活性主要在结直肠癌和小鼠模型中验证，在其他实体瘤中的普遍性有待进一步确认。
    - LAG3的诱导和增敏效果是在CT26（微卫星稳定）模型中观察到的，不能直接推广到所有MSI-H肿瘤或所有免疫检查点组合。
    - 核糖体状态签名是基于NMF算法构建的计算指标，虽经多项验证，但仍需结合其他独立方法确认其在不同平台数据中的稳健性。
##### Topic handles
    - 核糖体生物合成活性作为免疫细胞功能状态的标志物
    - HSP70+ CD8+ T细胞（应激反应T细胞）在肿瘤微环境中的角色
    - MRC1+ / LA-TAMs（脂质相关/调节性肿瘤相关巨噬细胞）的敏感性
    - CX-5461介导的TME重编程机制
    - LAG3作为核糖体靶向治疗增敏的联合靶点
    - 核糖体状态与免疫检查点抑制剂（ICB）疗效的相关性

#### Supplement material
    - **Auxiliary material:** The study utilized single-cell RNA sequencing (scRNA-seq) of murine tumors pre- and post-CX-5461 treatment, along with large-scale human scRNA-seq datasets from ICB-treated patients (lung cancer and CRC cohorts). It employed non-negative matrix factorization (NMF) to define ribosome-related programs and validated findings using multicolor immunostaining for rRNA (Y10B), HSP70, CD8, and MRC1. The research highlights that active ribosome biogenesis is associated with poor prognosis and immunosuppressive phenotypes in various solid tumors. It also notes that CX-5461 induces misfolded proteins, potentially creating neoantigens, although this mechanism was not directly linked to specific metabolite quantification.
    - **Gap id:** specific_metabolite_quantification
    - **Judgment path:** F:\OptoMind-Review-2\outputs\progressive_review_plan\20260924_microbiome\run01\level1\supplements\specific_metabolite_quantification\source_units\SU02_search_candidate_16ed5874ed\FULFILLMENT_JUDGMENT.json
    - **Material ready:** True
    - **Material usefulness:** supporting
    - **Outline action:** Identify studies that perform targeted metabolomics (LC-MS/GC-MS) on human tumor biopsies or serum from patients undergoing ICI therapy. Prioritize papers that correlate specific metabolite levels (inosine, SCFAs, amino acids, lipids) with clinical response (ORR, PFS, OS) or immune infiltration profiles. Ensure the selected material explicitly reports concentration values or relative abundance changes linked to outcome stratification.
    - **Outline action recovered by policy:** False
    - **Remaining gap:** The gap question requires data linking specific physiological concentrations of metabolites (e.g., inosine, SCFAs) in human serum or tumor tissue to Immune Checkpoint Inhibitor (ICI) outcomes. This study does not measure or report the concentrations of these specific small-molecule metabolites. Instead, it focuses on cellular ribosome biogenesis activity (a translational/protein synthesis metric) and immune cell phenotypic states. While it provides mechanistic insights into how host cell metabolic machinery (ribosomes) influences immunotherapy response, it lacks the direct quantitative metabolomic data required to fulfill the success criterion.
    - **Remaining gap recovered by policy:** False
    - **Status:** unmet
    - **Useful material:** This study investigated the impact of ribosome biogenesis blockade using CX-5461 on the tumor microenvironment (TME) in immunocompetent mouse models (MC38, CT26) and human clinical samples. The authors found that ribosome inhibition reduced the abundance of immunosuppressive cell populations, specifically HSP70+ CD8+ T cells (stress-response T cells), regulatory T cells (Tregs), and MRC1+ (LA/Reg-TAMs) macrophages, which were characterized by high cellular ribosome biogenesis activity. Conversely, it increased cytotoxic granule secretion in T/NK cells and pro-inflammatory markers in macrophages. The study also demonstrated that CX-5461 treatment induced the expression of immune checkpoints, particularly LAG3, in CD8+ T cells, and sensitized tumors to anti-Lag3 immunotherapy. Additionally, the authors developed a 73-metagene 'ribosomal state' signature to quantify ribosome biogenesis levels at single-cell resolution.

### Safety and efficacy of fecal microbiota transplantation in solid cancers resistant to immune checkpoint inhibitors: results of the MITRIC trial

- Source handle: `P0578`
- Paper ID: `d9577b3a29bd8aec60c9e27c6a56a63faf3f4a27`
- DOI: 10.1136/jitc-2026-015122

  - **Available material depth:** fulltext

#### Paper understanding from a
    - **Approach:** 采用单臂、开放标签的IIa期篮子试验设计。纳入标准包括ICI治疗后疾病进展、ECOG评分0-1及可测量病灶。供体需为ICI长期响应者（>12个月响应）。干预措施包括基线后第1周和第5周通过结肠镜进行两次FMT，随后在第14、30、78周可通过灌肠进行最多三次补充FMT。ICI治疗保持不变或重新引入。主要终点评估安全性和ORR（基于iRECIST）。次要终点包括可行性、临床获益率、无进展生存期（PFS）、总生存期（OS）、微生物定植（16S rRNA测序）、免疫反应（质谱流式细胞术分析PBMCs）及生物标志物（血清细胞因子）。统计分析采用描述性统计、Kaplan-Meier生存分析及Cox比例风险模型探索免疫特征与预后的关联，未进行多重检验校正。
##### Contribution and limits
    - **1.**
      - **Contribution:** 证实了在ICI耐药的晚期实体瘤患者中，使用ICI响应者供体的FMT是安全和可行的。提供了关于FMT后微生物定植动力学（短暂性）和系统性免疫景观（髓系抑制与T细胞活化）的详细生物标志物数据。
      - **边界与限制:** 样本量小（n=12），试验因入组缓慢提前终止。单臂设计无法确立因果关系，缺乏对照组。未能实现预期的临床疗效（无ORR），限制了对其机制解释的确定性。结果可能受到快速进展性疾病背景的影响，未能在足够长的时间内观察FMT的潜在延迟效应。
##### Key findings
    - **1.**
      - **适用条件:** 12名ICI耐药晚期实体瘤患者，接受来自ICI响应者的FMT联合ICI治疗
      - **研究发现:** FMT安全性良好，无严重FMT相关不良事件导致停药，但50%患者出现免疫相关不良事件（irAEs），多数为轻度。客观缓解率（ORR）为0%，中位PFS为1.5个月，中位OS为10.1个月。
    - **2.**
      - **适用条件:** 微生物组分析
      - **研究发现:** 首次FMT后一周，大多数受体的微生物组向供体方向偏移（定植成功），但这种相似性在后续时间点大多下降，表明定植多为暂时性。仅1名获得临床获益的患者实现了持久的供体-受体微生物相似性。
    - **3.**
      - **适用条件:** 免疫微环境分析
      - **研究发现:** 基线时高度分化的T细胞环境（CD8+效应记忆T细胞和TEMRA）与改善的PFS和OS相关；而髓系偏倚环境和幼稚T细胞表型预后较差。CD14+单核细胞和血清IL-8水平随时间增加，高水平的CD14+单核细胞与较短的PFS和OS相关。这些免疫特征与预后的关联在使用ICI治疗起始日期重新计算时依然成立，提示其反映的是宿主对免疫治疗的固有反应能力，而非FMT的直接因果效应。
    - **Paper kind:** empirical
    - **Problem or question:** 早期研究表明FMT可能逆转ICI耐药，但其适用性在其他肿瘤类型中尚不明确，且关于患者选择、供体选择及给药方案的问题仍未解决。本研究试图回答：在ICI耐药的晚期实体瘤患者中，重复FMT是否安全可行，以及是否能通过微生物定植和免疫重塑来恢复ICI的疗效。
    - **Research scope:** 针对晚期实体瘤（黑色素瘤、头颈部鳞癌、肾细胞癌、微卫星不稳定性高胰腺癌）中免疫检查点抑制剂（ICI）耐药或进展的患者，评估来自ICI长期响应者的粪菌移植（FMT）联合ICI治疗的安全性和有效性。
    - **Work summary:** 本研究是一项单臂IIa期篮子试验（MITRIC），旨在验证通过FMT调节肠道微生物群能否克服已建立的ICI耐药性。研究纳入了12名在转移性阶段对ICI治疗进展的患者，使用ICI长期响应者作为供体，给予患者最多五次FMT（前两次通过结肠镜，后续通过灌肠），并继续原有ICI治疗。主要终点为FMT相关不良事件和客观缓解率（ORR）。结果显示FMT安全性良好，但未能观察到客观肿瘤反应（ORR为0%），仅5名患者达到疾病稳定。尽管缺乏总体疗效证据，研究发现FMT诱导了短暂的微生物定植，且外周血中的免疫细胞特征（如分化T细胞与髓系细胞的平衡）与生存期显著相关，但这些免疫特征似乎更多反映疾病本身状态而非FMT的特异性干预效果。

#### Review planning account from b
##### Broader review uses
    - **1.**
      - **Connection to review:** 提供FMT在临床转化中的实际挑战案例，特别是供体选择和患者异质性问题。
      - **Material:** 研究讨论了使用ICI响应者作为供体的局限性（微生物组成差异大），以及不同研究间供体数量（单供体vs多供体）对结果的潜在影响。
      - **本节作用:** 用于讨论“供体选择标准”对FMT疗效的影响，指出单一供体或多供体混合策略的优劣尚无共识，且供体与受体的匹配度可能是关键因素。
    - **2.**
      - **Connection to review:** 提供关于FMT给药途径和频率的可行性数据。
      - **Material:** 比较了结肠镜给药（前两次）和灌肠给药（后续）的可行性及耐受性。
      - **本节作用:** 用于综述中关于“FMT最佳给药方案”的部分，说明多次、重复给药在技术上可行，但在临床实践中受限于患者状况和疾病进展。
##### Facet contributions
    - **1.**
      - **Boundaries:** 仅适用于ICI耐药/进展人群，不能外推至一线治疗场景。样本量极小，结果可能存在偶然性，但趋势明确。
      - **Contribution:** 提供了FMT在广泛ICI耐药实体瘤队列中的真实世界疗效数据：ORR为0%，中位PFS仅为1.5个月。这表明在非选择性、快速进展的耐药人群中，FMT联合ICI并非有效的挽救疗法。
      - **Facet id:** F1
      - **Possible uses:** 用于支持综述中关于“FMT疗效具有高度情境依赖性”或“需严格筛选患者群体”的论点。对比其他阳性研究，说明患者基线状态（如疾病负荷、进展速度）对FMT结局的关键影响。
    - **2.**
      - **Boundaries:** 免疫关联分析为探索性，未校正多重检验，且缺乏对照组，无法证明FMT改变了这些免疫特征从而改善了预后。仅描述了相关性，未证明因果机制。
      - **Contribution:** 揭示了FMT干预后系统性免疫景观的变化及其与预后的关联：(1) FMT诱导的微生物定植多为暂时性，仅持久定植者与临床获益相关；(2) 基线高分化T细胞表型预示更好预后，而高髓系细胞（CD14+）和高IL-8水平预示不良预后；(3) 这些免疫特征似乎反映了宿主固有的免疫反应能力，而非FMT直接重塑的结果。
      - **Facet id:** F2
      - **Possible uses:** 用于解释FMT起效的潜在机制（需要持久定植和特定的宿主免疫背景）。提供生物标志物候选项（如CD14+单核细胞、IL-8、T细胞分化状态）以预测哪些患者可能从微生物调节中受益。帮助区分“药物直接效应”与“宿主背景效应”。
    - **Planning summary:** 该论文提供了关于FMT在ICI耐药实体瘤中应用的阴性疗效结果及详细的转化医学发现。对于综述而言，其核心价值在于反驳了“FMT能普遍逆转ICI耐药”的简单假设，并指出了患者选择（如疾病进展速度、既往免疫状态）和供体匹配的重要性。同时，它提供了关于肠道微生物定植持久性与临床获益之间潜在联系的生物学见解，以及系统性免疫景观（特别是髓系细胞和T细胞亚群）作为预后生物标志物的证据。
##### Scope interpretation cautions
    - 该研究的阴性结果主要归因于患者群体的快速进展特性和小样本量，不应将其解读为FMT在所有情况下均无效，而应视为特定情境下的失败尝试。
    - 免疫生物标志物的发现是探索性的，且可能与ICI治疗本身的效应混淆，需谨慎解读其作为FMT特异性机制的作用。
    - 微生物定植的暂时性并不必然意味着临床无效，因为唯一获益患者的持久定植提示了两者间的潜在联系，但这仅是个案观察。
##### Topic handles
    - fecal microbiota transplantation (FMT)
    - immune checkpoint inhibitors (ICIs) resistance
    - solid tumors (melanoma, HNSCC, RCC, pancreatic cancer)
    - microbiota engraftment kinetics
    - peripheral blood mononuclear cells (PBMCs)
    - myeloid-derived suppressor cells / CD14+ monocytes
    - T-cell differentiation phenotypes
    - serum cytokines (IL-8)

#### Supplement material
    - **Auxiliary material:** The study provides mechanistic insights into why efficacy may be limited in certain contexts. It found that microbiota engraftment from donors was transient in most recipients, with durable engraftment observed only in the single patient who achieved clinical benefit. Mass cytometry analysis revealed that a baseline myeloid-dominant environment (high CD14+ monocytes) and high serum IL-8 were associated with poorer outcomes, while an activated T-cell signature was favorable. These immune features appeared to reflect the host's underlying disease state rather than being directly modified by FMT to improve outcomes. Additionally, the review planning material notes that this study highlights challenges in donor selection (using ICI responders vs healthy donors) and patient heterogeneity (rapidly progressive disease vs stable disease), suggesting that FMT efficacy is highly context-dependent.

    - **Material ready:** True
    - **Material usefulness:** supporting
    - **Outline action:** Identify and retrieve full-text or registry data for actual Phase III randomized controlled trials of FMT or defined microbial consortia in oncology (e.g., TACITO, though noted as randomized, verify if Phase III; other ongoing/completed Phase III trials like those in melanoma or RCC). If no such trials are found in the literature base, explicitly state that no Phase III RCTs have yet confirmed efficacy, using this Phase IIa negative result to illustrate the current stage of evidence (pre-registration/early-phase focus).
    - **Outline action recovered by policy:** False
    - **Remaining gap:** The gap question asks for confirmation of completed large-scale Phase III randomized controlled trials confirming microbiome intervention efficacy. This study is explicitly identified as a 'single-arm phase IIa' trial. It is neither Phase III nor randomized. Therefore, it does not fulfill the success criterion of providing data from a completed Phase III RCT.
    - **Remaining gap recovered by policy:** False
    - **Status:** unmet
    - **Useful material:** This study reports the results of the MITRIC trial (NCT05286294), a single-arm, phase IIa basket trial evaluating Fecal Microbiota Transplantation (FMT) from immune checkpoint inhibitor (ICI) responders in patients with advanced cancers progressing on ICIs. The trial enrolled 12 patients across melanoma, head and neck squamous cell carcinoma (HNSCC), renal cell carcinoma (RCC), and microsatellite instability-high pancreatic cancer. The co-primary endpoints were safety and objective response rate (ORR). The study found that FMT was safe and feasible but demonstrated limited clinical activity: no objective responses were observed (ORR 0%), median progression-free survival was 1.5 months, and median overall survival was 10.1 months. Clinical benefit (stable disease >6 months) was achieved in only one patient. The authors conclude that FMT does not support efficacy as a salvage therapy in this non-biomarker-driven, heavily pretreated population.

##### Safety and efficacy of fecal microbiota transplantation in solid cancers resistant to immune checkpoint inhibitors: results of the MITRIC trial
      - **Canonical paper id:** d9577b3a29bd8aec60c9e27c6a56a63faf3f4a27
      - **Doi:** 10.1136/jitc-2026-015122
    - **Source hash:** 163af5f925ccdf303a5e1ea759ee09e197370c5f96ebb2b13c0ffe191f601130
    - **Task id:** dr-task-d5ed1ee74fa77146cbbbb890
    - **Paper title:** Safety and efficacy of fecal microbiota transplantation in solid cancers resistant to immune checkpoint inhibitors: results of the MITRIC trial
##### Question material
    - **1.**
###### Examples
      - **1.**
      - **Attribution:** 本研究
      - **适用条件:** 研究对象为 ICI 治疗期间或治疗后进展的晚期实体瘤患者；供体为长期 ICI 响应者；干预措施为结肠镜和灌肠给予的重复 FMT 联合持续或重新引入的 ICI 治疗；评估标准采用 iRECIST。
      - **研究发现:** MITRIC 试验为一项单臂 IIa 期篮子试验，纳入 12 名对免疫检查点抑制剂（ICI）耐药的晚期实体瘤患者（黑色素瘤 n=9, ccRCC n=1, HNSCC n=1, MSI-H 胰腺癌 n=1）。主要终点客观缓解率（ORR）为 0/12（0%），未观察到任何客观缓解（部分或完全缓解）。5 名患者达到最佳稳定疾病（SD），7 名患者进展（PD）。中位无进展生存期（PFS）为 1.5 个月，中位总生存期（OS）为 10.1 个月。仅 1 名黑色素瘤患者获得协议定义的临床获益（SD >6 个月），另 2 名患者出现混合反应（部分病灶消退）。
      - **Use in review:** 提供 FMT 在 ICI 耐药/难治性实体瘤患者中的具体疗效数据（ORR, PFS, OS），并展示阴性结果的具体表现，作为该人群疗效局限性的直接证据。
      - **Explanation:** 作者对 MITRIC 试验结果为阴性的解释主要集中在患者选择、疾病特征及供体匹配等方面：
1. **患者选择与疾病进展速度**：既往研究（如 Baruch et al., Davar et al.）中的响应往往发生较晚，且有时 preceded by pseudo-progression（假性进展）。而 MITRIC 中的大多数患者表现为弥漫性、快速生长的疾病，可能没有足够的时间让 FMT 发挥有意义的生物学效应。
2. **供体匹配与异质性**：虽然既往研究中所有响应者均接受单一供体的粪便，而本试验使用了三名供体，可能存在次优的供体-受体匹配或缺乏产生获益所需的特定微生物群落。此外，ICI 响应者之间的微生物组成差异很大，使用健康供体是否更优尚不清楚。
3. **免疫微环境限制**：基线时髓系偏倚环境和幼稚 T 细胞表型与较差预后相关，且 FMT 未能逆转这种不利的髓系主导免疫环境（事实上 CD14+ 单核细胞和 IL-8 随时间增加，这与较短的 PFS 和 OS 相关）。这表明 FMT 可能仅在选定的具有特定免疫特征的人群中有效。
4. **样本量与异质性**：小样本量是重要局限性，且不同研究在供体选择、FMT 制备、给药途径和治疗方案上的异质性也可能影响结果。
      - **Question id:** Q01
###### Remaining points
      - FMT 在 ICI 耐药人群中的最佳生物标志物筛选标准尚未确立。
      - 供体选择（ICI 响应者 vs 健康供体）及个体化匹配策略对疗效的具体影响仍需进一步研究。
      - FMT 是否能通过改变外周血免疫表型（如减少髓系抑制）来增强疗效，目前数据不支持其诱导了显著的全身免疫改变。
    - **2.**
      - **Question id:** Q01
###### Examples
      - **1.**
      - **Attribution:** 本研究 (MITRIC trial)
      - **Use in review:** 提供阴性结果的详细解释，特别是关于患者选择（快速进展、晚期）、供体匹配（多供体 vs 单供体）及免疫微环境限制（髓系主导、IL-8升高），用于平衡Ch5中的阳性结果。
      - **研究发现:** 在12名ICI耐药实体瘤患者中，FMT联合ICI治疗未观察到客观缓解（ORR 0%）。尽管FMT安全且可行，但临床获益有限。机制分析显示，基线时髓系偏倚环境和幼稚T细胞表型与较差预后相关；治疗后CD14+单核细胞和血清IL-8水平升高，这与较短的PFS和OS相关，表明FMT未能逆转这种不利的免疫微环境，甚至可能伴随髓系驱动的免疫抑制增加。
      - **适用条件:** 研究对象为12名接受过多种治疗的晚期实体瘤患者（9例黑色素瘤，1例HNSCC，1例ccRCC，1例MSI-H胰腺癌），均处于ICI治疗后的疾病进展期。供体为3名长期ICI反应者。干预措施包括结肠镜给药的初始FMT和灌肠后续FMT。
      - **2.**
      - **Attribution:** Baruch et al. (2021)
      - **Use in review:** 作为对比背景，展示阳性结果中使用的单一供体策略和特定患者群体，以突显MITRIC试验中多供体和不同患者选择带来的差异。
      - **研究发现:** 在一项针对抗PD-1耐受的转移性黑色素瘤患者的概念验证试验中，使用粪便微生物移植（FMT）实现了3/10患者的客观缓解，另有3名患者获得持久的疾病稳定。
      - **适用条件:** 研究对象为抗PD-1耐受的转移性黑色素瘤患者。所有反应者均接受来自单一供体的粪便。
###### Reference ids
      - R19
      - **3.**
      - **Attribution:** Davar et al. (2021)
      - **Use in review:** 作为对比背景，展示阳性结果中观察到的免疫学变化（如CD56+CD8+ T细胞增加、IL-8降低），与MITRIC试验中的阴性结果形成机制上的对比。
      - **研究发现:** 在一项针对抗PD-1耐受的转移性黑色素瘤患者的试验中，FMT使3/15患者产生反应，另有3名患者获得持久的疾病稳定。响应者在FMT/抗PD-1治疗后表现出外周血中CD56+CD8+ T细胞水平升高，且这些细胞表达更高的激活标记物；同时，响应者体内循环IL-8降低，肿瘤内产生IL-8的髓系细胞频率减少。
      - **适用条件:** 研究对象为抗PD-1耐受的转移性黑色素瘤患者。
###### Reference ids
      - R18
      - **4.**
      - **Attribution:** Kim et al. (2024)
      - **Use in review:** 作为对比背景，展示在其他实体瘤（上消化道癌）中FMT的潜在疗效，说明癌症类型和疾病状态对结果的影响。
      - **研究发现:** 在抗PD-1抑制剂耐药的不可切除或转移性实体瘤患者中，FMT改善了1/13名上消化道癌症患者的抗PD-1抑制剂疗效。
      - **适用条件:** 研究对象为抗PD-1抑制剂耐药的不可切除或转移性实体瘤患者，主要为上消化道癌症。
###### Reference ids
      - R20
      - **Explanation:** MITRIC试验的阴性结果提示，FMT在克服ICI耐药方面并非普遍有效，其效果受到患者选择、供体匹配和免疫微环境状态的严格限制。

首先，患者选择是关键因素。与之前报告阳性结果的试验（如Baruch等和Davar等）相比，MITRIC试验中的患者大多患有扩散迅速、生长快速的疾病，这可能没有给予FMT足够的时间来发挥有意义的生物学效应。此外，MITRIC试验纳入了原发性和继发性ICI耐药患者，而之前的研究主要关注继发性耐药或特定类型的耐药，这可能导致了结果的异质性。

其次，供体匹配和多样性可能影响疗效。MITRIC试验使用了三名不同的供体，而Baruch等人的研究中所有反应者均来自单一供体。这表明，使用多名供体可能导致供体-受体匹配次优，或者缺乏对特定获益至关重要的微生物群落。供体的微生物组成存在显著差异，且目前尚不清楚使用ICI反应者作为供体是否优于健康供体。

最后，免疫微环境的限制是重要的机制解释。MITRIC试验发现，基线时髓系偏倚环境和幼稚T细胞表型与较差的预后相关。治疗后，CD14+单核细胞和血清IL-8水平升高，这与较短的PFS和OS相关。相比之下，Davar等人的研究发现响应者体内IL-8降低，且髓系细胞产生的IL-8减少。这表明，在MITRIC试验的患者群体中，FMT未能逆转由髓系驱动的免疫抑制微环境，甚至可能伴随着这种抑制的增加，从而限制了临床获益。因此，FMT可能仅在具有特定免疫特征（如高分化T细胞环境、低髓系浸润）的选定人群中有效，而可靠的生物标志物尚未建立。
###### Remaining points
      - MITRIC试验样本量小，且为单臂研究，无法进行因果推断或多重检验校正，因此关于免疫微环境和生物标志物的发现应谨慎解读。
      - 长期定植的效果难以评估，因为大多数患者在疾病进展后无法收集后续的粪便样本。
      - 需要进一步的研究来确定FMT的最佳给药方案、供体选择标准以及能够预测获益的患者亚群。

首先，患者选择是关键因素。与之前报告阳性结果的试验（如Baruch等和Davar等）相比，MITRIC试验中的患者大多患有扩散迅速、生长快速的疾病，这可能没有给予FMT足够的时间来发挥有意义的生物学效应。此外，MITRIC试验纳入了原发性和继发性ICI耐药患者，而之前的研究主要关注继发性耐药或特定类型的耐药，这可能导致了结果的异质性。

其次，供体匹配和多样性可能影响疗效。MITRIC试验使用了三名不同的供体，而Baruch等人的研究中所有反应者均来自单一供体。这表明，使用多名供体可能导致供体-受体匹配次优，或者缺乏对特定获益至关重要的微生物群落。供体的微生物组成存在显著差异，且目前尚不清楚使用ICI反应者作为供体是否优于健康供体。

最后，免疫微环境的限制是重要的机制解释。MITRIC试验发现，基线时髓系偏倚环境和幼稚T细胞表型与较差的预后相关。治疗后，CD14+单核细胞和血清IL-8水平升高，这与较短的PFS和OS相关。相比之下，Davar等人的研究发现响应者体内IL-8降低，且髓系细胞产生的IL-8减少。这表明，在MITRIC试验的患者群体中，FMT未能逆转由髓系驱动的免疫抑制微环境，甚至可能伴随着这种抑制的增加，从而限制了临床获益。因此，FMT可能仅在具有特定免疫特征（如高分化T细胞环境、低髓系浸润）的选定人群中有效，而可靠的生物标志物尚未建立。
###### Remaining points
      - MITRIC试验样本量小，且为单臂研究，无法进行因果推断或多重检验校正，因此关于免疫微环境和生物标志物的发现应谨慎解读。
      - 长期定植的效果难以评估，因为大多数患者在疾病进展后无法收集后续的粪便样本。
      - 需要进一步的研究来确定FMT的最佳给药方案、供体选择标准以及能够预测获益的患者亚群。
##### Prior question material
    - **1.**
###### Examples
      - **1.**
      - **Attribution:** 本研究
      - **适用条件:** 研究对象为 ICI 治疗期间或治疗后进展的晚期实体瘤患者；供体为长期 ICI 响应者；干预措施为结肠镜和灌肠给予的重复 FMT 联合持续或重新引入的 ICI 治疗；评估标准采用 iRECIST。
      - **研究发现:** MITRIC 试验为一项单臂 IIa 期篮子试验，纳入 12 名对免疫检查点抑制剂（ICI）耐药的晚期实体瘤患者（黑色素瘤 n=9, ccRCC n=1, HNSCC n=1, MSI-H 胰腺癌 n=1）。主要终点客观缓解率（ORR）为 0/12（0%），未观察到任何客观缓解（部分或完全缓解）。5 名患者达到最佳稳定疾病（SD），7 名患者进展（PD）。中位无进展生存期（PFS）为 1.5 个月，中位总生存期（OS）为 10.1 个月。仅 1 名黑色素瘤患者获得协议定义的临床获益（SD >6 个月），另 2 名患者出现混合反应（部分病灶消退）。
      - **Use in review:** 提供 FMT 在 ICI 耐药/难治性实体瘤患者中的具体疗效数据（ORR, PFS, OS），并展示阴性结果的具体表现，作为该人群疗效局限性的直接证据。
      - **Explanation:** 作者对 MITRIC 试验结果为阴性的解释主要集中在患者选择、疾病特征及供体匹配等方面：
1. **患者选择与疾病进展速度**：既往研究（如 Baruch et al., Davar et al.）中的响应往往发生较晚，且有时 preceded by pseudo-progression（假性进展）。而 MITRIC 中的大多数患者表现为弥漫性、快速生长的疾病，可能没有足够的时间让 FMT 发挥有意义的生物学效应。
2. **供体匹配与异质性**：虽然既往研究中所有响应者均接受单一供体的粪便，而本试验使用了三名供体，可能存在次优的供体-受体匹配或缺乏产生获益所需的特定微生物群落。此外，ICI 响应者之间的微生物组成差异很大，使用健康供体是否更优尚不清楚。
3. **免疫微环境限制**：基线时髓系偏倚环境和幼稚 T 细胞表型与较差预后相关，且 FMT 未能逆转这种不利的髓系主导免疫环境（事实上 CD14+ 单核细胞和 IL-8 随时间增加，这与较短的 PFS 和 OS 相关）。这表明 FMT 可能仅在选定的具有特定免疫特征的人群中有效。
4. **样本量与异质性**：小样本量是重要局限性，且不同研究在供体选择、FMT 制备、给药途径和治疗方案上的异质性也可能影响结果。
      - **Question id:** Q01
###### Remaining points
      - FMT 在 ICI 耐药人群中的最佳生物标志物筛选标准尚未确立。
      - 供体选择（ICI 响应者 vs 健康供体）及个体化匹配策略对疗效的具体影响仍需进一步研究。
      - FMT 是否能通过改变外周血免疫表型（如减少髓系抑制）来增强疗效，目前数据不支持其诱导了显著的全身免疫改变。

### Gut microbiota shapes cancer immunotherapy responses

- Source handle: `P0146`
- Paper ID: `4171288ecca93c6bca3eee61295418d704bb00fd`
- DOI: 10.1038/s41522-025-00786-8

  - **Available material depth:** fulltext

#### Paper understanding from a
    - **Approach:** As a narrative review, this paper does not employ a systematic search protocol described in the text. Instead, it synthesizes findings from a broad range of sources including: 1) Historical timeline analysis of key advancements; 2) Summary of clinical trial data (Tables 1-5) linking specific microbial taxa to ICI/ACT outcomes; 3) Mechanistic discussion based on preclinical models (mouse studies) and human cohort analyses regarding immune cell activation, metabolite pathways (SCFAs, bile acids, etc.), and intratumoral interactions; 4) Evaluation of intervention strategies (antibiotics, FMT, probiotics, diet, engineered microbes) by summarizing results from ongoing and completed clinical trials.
##### Contribution and limits
    - **1.**
      - **Contribution:** Provides a structured synthesis of clinical and preclinical evidence linking specific gut microbial taxa to ICI and ACT outcomes across multiple cancer types. It categorizes mechanisms into innate/adaptive immunity modulation, metabolite effects, and intratumoral interactions. It also catalogs numerous ongoing clinical trials for FMT, probiotics, and dietary interventions.
      - **边界与限制:** As a narrative review, it lacks a systematic methodology for study selection, potentially introducing selection bias. Many findings are derived from small-scale clinical studies or animal models, limiting generalizability. The field faces significant heterogeneity in sequencing methods, sample handling, and lack of standardized protocols, making cross-study comparisons difficult. Causal relationships between specific microbes and clinical outcomes are often correlational rather than proven causal.
##### Key findings
    - **1.**
      - **适用条件:** ICI therapy (anti-PD-1/PD-L1/CTLA-4)
      - **研究发现:** Specific gut microbial compositions correlate with ICI response. Beneficial taxa include Bifidobacterium, Akkermansia muciniphila, Ruminococcaceae, and Faecalibacterium prausnitzii, which are associated with improved survival and tumor regression in melanoma, NSCLC, and RCC. Conversely, antibiotic use generally reduces ICI efficacy.
    - **2.**
      - **适用条件:** ACT therapy (CAR-T, TCR-T)
      - **研究发现:** Gut microbiota influences ACT efficacy and toxicity. Certain microbes (e.g., Ruminococcus, Bacteroides) are linked to CAR-T efficacy, while others may predict neurotoxicity or cytokine release syndrome (CRS). Microbial metabolites like SCFAs can enhance CD8+ T cell cytotoxicity.
    - **3.**
      - **适用条件:** Mechanisms of Immune Modulation
      - **研究发现:** Microbiota modulate immunity via direct interaction with dendritic cells and macrophages (PRR activation), metabolite signaling (SCFAs, bile acids, tryptophan metabolites influencing T cell differentiation), and molecular mimicry (cross-reactive T cells). Intratumoral microbiota can remodel the TME, promoting TLS formation or inducing immunosuppression.
    - **4.**
      - **适用条件:** Intervention Strategies
      - **研究发现:** FMT from ICI responders can overcome resistance in refractory melanoma patients. Probiotics (e.g., Clostridium butyricum CBM588) and dietary interventions (high-fiber, ketogenic) show promise in enhancing ICI response and reducing toxicity, though clinical evidence is still emerging.
    - **Paper kind:** review
    - **Problem or question:** Despite the success of immunotherapies like ICIs and ACTs, patient responses vary widely due to primary or acquired resistance and adverse events. This review aims to elucidate the role of the gut microbiota in shaping these outcomes, identify potential biomarkers for predicting response, and explore microbiota-based strategies to enhance efficacy and mitigate toxicity.
    - **Research scope:** The review focuses on the gut microbiota and its influence on cancer immunotherapy, specifically immune checkpoint inhibitors (ICIs) and adoptive cell therapies (ACTs). It covers clinical correlations, preclinical mechanisms, intratumoral microbiota, and intervention strategies across various solid tumors and hematologic malignancies.
    - **Work summary:** This paper provides a comprehensive overview of how the gut microbiome modulates the efficacy and toxicity of cancer immunotherapies. It traces the historical evolution from early animal studies to recent clinical trials, detailing specific microbial signatures associated with response or resistance to ICIs and ACTs. The authors synthesize mechanistic insights involving innate and adaptive immune modulation, metabolite production, and tumor microenvironment remodeling. Furthermore, it evaluates therapeutic interventions such as fecal microbiota transplantation (FMT), probiotics, dietary modifications, and engineered bacteria, while highlighting challenges in standardization and clinical translation.

#### Review planning account from b
##### Broader review uses
    - **1.**
      - **Connection to review:** Contextual background on the evolution of microbiome-immunotherapy research
      - **Material:** Historical perspective section tracing discoveries from 2007 to 2025, highlighting the shift from correlation to mechanistic understanding and clinical trials.
      - **本节作用:** To provide a timeline of key advancements and justify the current interest in microbiome modulation as a critical factor in immunotherapy success.
    - **2.**
      - **Connection to review:** Discussion of intervention strategies beyond just biomarkers
      - **Material:** Sections on FMT, probiotics, antibiotics, diet, and engineered microbes, including tables of clinical trials.
      - **本节作用:** To illustrate potential therapeutic avenues for overcoming ICI resistance and managing toxicity, providing examples of how microbiome modulation is being translated into clinical practice.
    - **3.**
      - **Connection to review:** Challenges and limitations in the field
      - **Material:** Section on 'Challenges and future directions' discussing technical variability, donor effects in FMT, host genetics, and lack of standardization.
      - **本节作用:** To highlight methodological hurdles and confounding factors that must be considered when interpreting microbiome-ICI associations, explaining why consistent biomarkers are hard to identify.
##### Facet contributions
    - **1.**
      - **Boundaries:** Associations are largely correlative from retrospective or small prospective cohorts. Specificity varies by cancer type and ICI agent. Lack of standardized detection methods limits direct comparison. Does not establish causality.
      - **Contribution:** Identifies specific gut microbial taxa associated with ICI response or resistance in solid tumors. For example, Akkermansia muciniphila, Bifidobacterium, and Ruminococcaceae are linked to better outcomes in melanoma and NSCLC, while certain Proteobacteria or high abundance of Bacteroides intestinalis may correlate with resistance or toxicity. It also notes that beneficial taxa vary by ICI type (anti-PD-1 vs. anti-CTLA-4) and cancer type.
      - **Facet id:** F1
      - **Possible uses:** Can be used to list candidate microbial biomarkers for predicting ICI efficacy in solid tumors. Helps explain inter-patient variability in treatment response.
    - **2.**
      - **Boundaries:** Many mechanisms are primarily demonstrated in mouse models (preclinical). Human validation is often indirect or observational. Some mechanisms (e.g., molecular mimicry) are proposed but require further confirmation in humans. Effects can be bidirectional (promoting or suppressing immunity depending on context).
      - **Contribution:** Details mechanisms by which gut microbiota modulate ICI efficacy: 1) Direct immune modulation via PRR activation (TLR/NLR) leading to DC maturation and T-cell activation; 2) Metabolite-mediated regulation (SCFAs inhibiting HDAC, bile acids affecting Th17/Treg balance, tryptophan metabolites via AhR); 3) Molecular mimicry where bacterial antigens cross-react with tumor antigens; 4) Remodeling of the tumor microenvironment (TME) through intratumoral bacteria or systemic metabolites influencing tertiary lymphoid structures (TLS).
      - **Facet id:** F2
      - **Possible uses:** Provides mechanistic explanations for how the microbiome influences ICI response. Supports the biological rationale for microbiome-targeted interventions.
    - **Planning summary:** This review serves as a foundational resource for mapping the relationship between gut microbiome composition and ICI efficacy in solid tumors. It provides specific microbial markers (taxa) associated with response/resistance, details mechanistic pathways (metabolites, immune cell crosstalk), and outlines current intervention strategies (FMT, probiotics, diet). It is particularly useful for identifying candidate biomarkers and understanding the biological plausibility of microbiome-mediated modulation, while also highlighting the methodological challenges and variability that complicate definitive conclusions.
##### Scope interpretation cautions
    - The review covers both gut and intratumoral microbiota; ensure distinction is maintained when applying findings to 'gut microbiome' specific facets.
    - Findings on specific bacterial taxa are highly dependent on the cancer type (e.g., melanoma vs. NSCLC) and the specific ICI agent (anti-PD-1 vs. anti-CTLA-4); avoid over-generalizing across all solid tumors.
    - Many mechanistic insights are derived from murine models; caution is needed when extrapolating to human physiology without explicit human validation mentioned in the text.
    - Clinical trial data cited are often from early-phase (I/II) or small cohorts; results should not be interpreted as definitive proof of efficacy for interventions like FMT or probiotics.
##### Topic handles
    - gut microbiota composition and diversity
    - immune checkpoint inhibitors (ICIs)
    - solid tumors (melanoma, NSCLC, RCC, HCC, CRC)
    - fecal microbiota transplantation (FMT)
    - probiotics and prebiotics
    - dietary interventions (high-fiber, ketogenic)
    - microbial metabolites (SCFAs, bile acids, tryptophan metabolites)
    - intratumoral microbiota
    - predictive biomarkers for ICI response
    - immune-related adverse events (irAEs)

### Overcoming Resistance Mechanisms to Immune Checkpoint Inhibitors: Leveraging the Anti-Tumor Immune Response

- Source handle: `P0157`
- Paper ID: `45ca27020bec6c917c6b71d8b1bad217f5abd0ed`
- DOI: 10.3390/curroncol31010001

  - **Available material depth:** fulltext

#### Paper understanding from a
    - **Approach:** 作为一篇综述，本文采用逻辑分类法组织材料。首先基于免疫学基础（T细胞介导的免疫）建立背景框架；其次，依据免疫激活的时间顺序和空间位置（TME访问、启动、增效、维持）对耐药机制进行系统性归类；再次，针对每一类耐药机制，列举相应的克服策略（如联合化疗/放疗、靶向替代检查点、调节微生物群等）；最后，结合临床前模型和部分临床试验结果（如KEYNOTE系列、CheckMate系列等）来佐证各策略的有效性和局限性。
##### Contribution and limits
    - **1.**
      - **Contribution:** 提供了一个基于免疫激活连续阶段的系统性耐药机制框架，并将宿主因素（微生物群、药物）纳入整体考量。
      - **边界与限制:** 作为综述，其结论依赖于所引用的文献，未提供新的原始实验数据。部分策略的临床证据尚处于早期阶段（如I/II期试验），长期疗效和安全性有待进一步验证。
##### Key findings
    - **1.**
      - **适用条件:** 实体瘤（特别是黑色素瘤、非小细胞肺癌等），使用抗PD-1/PD-L1或抗CTLA-4治疗
      - **研究发现:** 耐药机制可分为四类：1. TME物理屏障（如CAF、异常血管）阻碍T细胞浸润；2. 抗原缺失或呈递缺陷导致免疫启动失败；3. 免疫抑制性细胞因子（如TGF-β）、代谢产物（乳酸）及替代检查点（TIM-3, LAG3, TIGIT）导致T细胞功能受损或耗竭；4. 记忆T细胞形成不足导致长期免疫监视失效。
    - **2.**
      - **适用条件:** 肠道微生物群状态
      - **研究发现:** 健康的肠道微生物群通过抗原交叉反应、诱导局部免疫调节细胞因子及分泌代谢物进入循环，促进抗肿瘤免疫反应。菌群失调（dysbiosis）或与抗生素、PPIs、类固醇等药物合用可能导致ICI疗效降低。FMT和益生菌显示出恢复ICI敏感性的潜力。
    - **3.**
      - **适用条件:** 联合治疗策略
      - **研究发现:** 联合抗VEGF疗法（如阿替利珠单抗+贝伐珠单抗）在NSCLC中显示出优于单药的PFS和OS。联合LAG3抑制剂（如Relatlimab）在黑色素瘤中改善了PFS。然而，某些组合（如PARPi+ICI在前列腺癌中，或MEKi+ICI在结直肠癌中）未能达到主要终点，提示需更精准的生物标志物筛选。
    - **Paper kind:** review
    - **Problem or question:** 尽管ICIs在某些癌症中取得了成功，但许多患者存在原发性无响应或获得性耐药。目前缺乏通用的预测生物标志物，且耐药机制复杂多样。本研究旨在厘清这些耐药机制，并评估当前及新兴的治疗策略以克服耐药，从而提高ICI的整体疗效。
    - **Research scope:** 免疫检查点抑制剂（ICIs）在实体瘤治疗中的应用，重点聚焦于原发性及获得性耐药机制，以及克服这些耐药性的潜在策略。
    - **Work summary:** 本文是一篇综述文章，旨在系统梳理T细胞介导的抗肿瘤免疫反应原理，分析癌症细胞对ICIs产生原发性或获得性耐药的多种机制，并探讨逆转这些耐药性的治疗策略。文章首先回顾了从Coley毒素到现代ICI的发展历程，随后详细阐述了T细胞激活、浸润肿瘤微环境（TME）、效应功能及维持免疫记忆的过程。在此基础上，文章将耐药机制按免疫激活的连续阶段进行分类：包括免疫细胞进入TME受阻、抗肿瘤免疫反应启动失败、效应功能减弱以及免疫反应无法持续。此外，还讨论了宿主因素如肠道微生物群和合并用药对ICI疗效的影响。最后，文章总结了多种克服耐药的策略，包括增强肿瘤免疫原性、改善免疫抑制性微环境、逆转T细胞耗竭、联合小分子抑制剂以及调节肠道微生物群等。

#### Review planning account from b
##### Broader review uses
    - **1.**
      - **Connection to review:** 虽然综述焦点是微生物群，但了解这些广泛的耐药机制有助于确定微生物群干预主要针对哪一环节（如改善TME免疫浸润或逆转T细胞耗竭）。
      - **Material:** 文章第3节详细列出了基于免疫激活阶段的耐药机制，包括TME物理屏障、抗原呈递缺陷、免疫抑制性细胞因子（TGF-β, IL-10）、代谢压力（缺氧、乳酸）以及替代检查点（TIM-3, LAG3, TIGIT）的上调。
      - **本节作用:** 背景知识：概述ICI耐药的多维机制
    - **2.**
      - **Connection to review:** 将微生物群调节与其他策略并列，有助于评估微生物群干预在综合治疗体系中的地位。例如，文中提到FMT与ICI联合已在多项试验中进行，而某些小分子抑制剂组合则失败了，这为比较不同干预路径的有效性提供了参考。
      - **Material:** 文章第4节比较了多种克服耐药的策略，包括联合化疗/放疗（增加免疫原性）、抗VEGF疗法（改善血管和免疫抑制）、靶向替代检查点（LAG3, TIM-3, TIGIT）以及细胞疗法（TILs, CAR-T）。
      - **本节作用:** 对比其他克服耐药策略的语境
##### Facet contributions
    - **1.**
      - **Boundaries:** 提供的临床数据多为小规模试点研究或回顾性分析，样本量有限。益生菌的具体菌株、剂量和最佳给药时机尚未标准化。
      - **Contribution:** 提供了肠道微生物群影响ICI疗效的具体临床观察和初步干预结果。例如，引用了一项转移性肾细胞癌患者的前瞻性研究，显示添加含丁酸梭菌的益生菌显著提高了客观缓解率（59% vs 11%）和中位PFS。另一项黑色素瘤研究显示，FMT联合ICI使ORR达到65%，且只有那些微生物群持续向供体靠拢的患者才产生响应。
      - **Facet id:** F1
      - **Possible uses:** 用于论证肠道微生物群组成与ICI疗效之间的正相关性，以及通过FMT或特定益生菌干预可以逆转耐药或增强初治响应的可能性。
    - **2.**
      - **Boundaries:** 部分机制（如Fap2-TIGIT相互作用）主要基于临床前模型或体外研究，其在人体内的确切贡献程度尚需更多临床验证。不同癌症类型中主导机制可能不同，文中未区分具体癌种的机制差异。
      - **Contribution:** 详细阐述了微生物群影响免疫系统的三条主要机制：1. 抗原交叉反应（Cross-reactivity）：针对细菌抗原致敏的T细胞可识别具有相同抗原表位的肿瘤细胞；2. 局部免疫调节：微生物诱导产生全身性扩散的免疫调节细胞因子，改变TME中的免疫细胞亚群分布；3. 代谢物调节：正常微生物群分泌的代谢物进入血液循环，影响Th细胞活化、抗体分泌及DC/巨噬细胞功能。此外，还提到了Fap2蛋白结合TIGIT触发抑制信号的潜在机制。
      - **Facet id:** F2
      - **Possible uses:** 用于解释肠道微生物群介导ICI疗效增强的生物学通路，为后续寻找具体的微生物标志物或代谢物靶点提供理论依据。
    - **3.**
      - **Boundaries:** 关于类固醇影响的因果关系存在不确定性，作者指出这可能反映的是患者本身较差的性能状态或肿瘤生物学特征，而非类固醇本身的直接药理作用。证据等级多为回顾性队列研究。
      - **Contribution:** 指出了干扰肠道微生物群的常见医疗行为（抗生素、PPIs、类固醇）对ICI疗效的负面影响。特别指出抗生素的使用时机可能很重要（治疗后使用可能不影响），而类固醇的使用指征（管理副作用 vs 改善症状）可能与疗效差异相关，但也可能是预后不良的表现。
      - **Facet id:** F1
      - **Possible uses:** 用于讨论混杂因素对ICI疗效评估的影响，以及在临床实践中如何管理合并用药以优化免疫治疗效果。
    - **Planning summary:** 该综述为理解肠道微生物群如何影响ICI疗效提供了详细的机制背景和临床证据支持。它明确指出了微生物群通过交叉反应、细胞因子诱导和代谢物调节三种途径影响免疫系统，并列举了FMT、益生菌和饮食干预等具体策略。同时，它也警告了抗生素、PPIs和类固醇等常见药物可能通过破坏微生物群平衡而削弱ICI疗效。这对于回答“肠道微生物群如何调节ICI疗效”及其“介导机制”至关重要。
##### Scope interpretation cautions
    - 本文是综述性质，其引用的临床试验结果（如KEYNOTE, CheckMate, IMpower150等）代表的是特定研究的设计和结果，不应视为对所有患者的普遍保证。
    - 关于微生物群与ICI疗效的关系，文中提到的机制多为假设性或基于动物模型/小规模人类研究的推断，尚未形成确凿的因果链条。
    - 对于合并用药（如类固醇）的影响，作者明确指出可能存在混杂因素（如患者基线状况），因此在解读时需保持谨慎，避免简单归因。
    - 不同癌症类型（如黑色素瘤、NSCLC、肾癌）对ICI的反应和耐药机制存在差异，文中虽提及但未深入区分每种机制在所有实体瘤中的普适性。

### Modulation of the response to immunotherapy in triple-negative breast cancer: the role of the microbiota and microbial metabolites in the tumor microenvironment

- Source handle: `P0177`
- Paper ID: `4bd0e5df8b16ec5b5019c46fe8b6ea8fed438f8c`
- DOI: 10.1080/19490976.2026.2697600

  - **Available material depth:** fulltext

#### Paper understanding from a
    - **Approach:** Narrative literature review conducted using PubMed, Google Scholar, and Web of Science (1998–2026). The authors screened titles/abstracts for studies aligned with the microbiota-metabolite-immune axis in TNBC, incorporating relevant broader breast cancer studies due to limited TNBC-specific data. Special emphasis was placed on contamination control in low-biomass tumor microbiome studies. The synthesis integrates clinical cohorts, preclinical models, and preliminary author data to stratify evidence and discuss mechanistic pathways.
##### Contribution and limits
    - **1.**
      - **Contribution:** Provides a comprehensive synthesis of the microbiota-metabolite-immune axis in TNBC, distinguishing between gut-derived systemic effects and intratumoral local effects. It offers a detailed catalog of metabolites (SCFAs, indoles, bile acids, toxins) and their proposed mechanisms, along with a critical evaluation of methodological limitations in low-biomass research.
      - **边界与限制:** The review is narrative and not systematic; it acknowledges that most evidence is extrapolated from other cancers or unstratified BC cohorts. Direct causal evidence in TNBC is scarce, and many findings are associative or preclinical. The review does not provide new experimental data but synthesizes existing fragmented evidence.
##### Key findings
    - **1.**
      - **适用条件:** Preclinical murine models and some clinical cohorts
      - **研究发现:** Akkermansia muciniphila enrichment correlates with better chemotherapy/ICI response via M1 macrophage polarization and CD8+ T-cell activation; conversely, Lactobacillus supplementation in some models promoted M2 polarization and tumor progression.
    - **2.**
      - **适用条件:** Clinical and preclinical studies
      - **研究发现:** Microbial metabolites like Trimethylamine N-oxide (TMAO) and Indole-3-propionic acid (IPA) are associated with improved ICI responses through mechanisms involving ER stress, pyroptosis, and cytotoxic T-cell activity.
    - **3.**
      - **适用条件:** Low-biomass tumor samples
      - **研究发现:** Intratumoral microbiome signals are highly susceptible to contamination artifacts; causality remains uncertain, and interpretation is limited by technical biases in sequencing and bioinformatics pipelines.
    - **4.**
      - **适用条件:** Gut microbiota composition
      - **研究发现:** Higher baseline alpha-diversity and specific taxa (e.g., Bacteroides eggerthii, Bifidobacterium longum) are associated with pathological complete response (pCR) to neoadjuvant therapy, while dysbiosis correlates with poor outcomes.
    - **Paper kind:** review
    - **Problem or question:** To identify extrinsic, modifiable determinants of antitumor immunity in TNBC, specifically addressing primary and acquired resistance to immune checkpoint inhibitors by elucidating the role of microbiota and microbial metabolites in shaping systemic and intratumoral immune tone.
    - **Research scope:** Triple-negative breast cancer (TNBC), focusing on the interaction between gut and tumor-associated microbiota, microbial metabolites, and immune checkpoint inhibitor (ICI) efficacy.
    - **Work summary:** This narrative review synthesizes current knowledge on how the microbiota-metabolite axis modulates antitumor immunity and response to chemoimmunotherapy in TNBC. It covers TNBC immunobiology, mammary and gut microbiome alterations, mechanistic pathways of microbial metabolites (including SCFAs, indoles, bile acids, and toxins), methodological challenges in low-biomass studies, and translational strategies such as probiotics, diet, FMT, and postbiotic approaches. The review emphasizes that while intratumoral effects are proposed, most clinically actionable evidence supports systemic gut-derived metabolites shaping the TNBC tumor microenvironment.

#### Review planning account from b
##### Broader review uses
    - **1.**
      - **Connection to review:** Helps frame why TNBC is a relevant model for studying microbiome-immunotherapy interactions due to its immunogenic nature and high unmet need.
      - **Material:** Overview of TNBC subtypes, high mutational burden, immunogenic features, and current standard of care (chemo-ICI).
      - **本节作用:** Contextual background on TNBC immunobiology and treatment landscape
    - **2.**
      - **Connection to review:** Provides critical perspective on interpreting microbiome data in oncology, emphasizing the need for standardized protocols and functional validation.
      - **Material:** Detailed discussion on contamination risks in low-biomass samples, need for rigorous controls, and heterogeneity in study designs.
      - **本节作用:** Methodological cautionary tale for microbiome research
    - **3.**
      - **Connection to review:** Offers insights into potential therapeutic interventions targeting the microbiome to enhance ICI efficacy, highlighting the gap between preclinical promise and clinical translation.
      - **Material:** Discussion of probiotics, prebiotics, FMT, dietary interventions, and postbiotic approaches, including ongoing clinical trials.
      - **本节作用:** Translational strategies and future directions
##### Facet contributions
    - **1.**
      - **Boundaries:** Most evidence is associative or from preclinical models; direct causal links in human TNBC patients are limited. The review explicitly states that causality remains uncertain for many findings.
      - **Contribution:** Summarizes clinical and preclinical evidence linking specific gut microbial signatures (e.g., Akkermansia, Bacteroides, Bifidobacterium) and higher alpha-diversity to improved response to ICIs and chemotherapy in TNBC. It also notes negative associations with antibiotic use and certain dysbiotic states.
      - **Facet id:** F1
      - **Possible uses:** Can be used to support arguments about the correlation between gut microbiome composition and ICI efficacy in solid tumors, particularly highlighting TNBC as a model where this link is being actively investigated.
    - **2.**
      - **Boundaries:** Many mechanisms are derived from preclinical models or extrapolated from other cancers. The review highlights context-dependency (e.g., butyrate paradox, Lactobacillus dual roles) and notes that direct validation in TNBC is often lacking.
      - **Contribution:** Details specific mechanistic pathways: 1) Metabolites like TMAO and IPA enhance CD8+ T-cell activity and induce immunogenic cell death/pyroptosis. 2) SCFAs (butyrate, propionate) act as HDAC inhibitors, affecting epigenetics and immune cell function. 3) Gut microbiota modulates systemic inflammation and myeloid polarization (M1/M2). 4) Intratumoral bacteria can directly influence local immune tone.
      - **Facet id:** F2
      - **Possible uses:** Provides concrete mechanistic examples of how gut microbiota influences ICI efficacy, including molecular targets (HDAC, TLRs, NLRP3) and immune cell subsets. Useful for explaining the biological plausibility of microbiome-immunotherapy interactions.
    - **Planning summary:** This review provides a structured overview of how gut and tumor microbiota influence ICI efficacy in solid tumors, with a specific focus on TNBC. It details mechanistic pathways involving microbial metabolites (SCFAs, indoles, bile acids, TMAO) and toxins, discusses methodological pitfalls in microbiome research, and outlines translational strategies. It serves as a source for mechanisms, biomarkers, and therapeutic interventions related to the gut microbiome's modulation of immunotherapy.
##### Scope interpretation cautions
    - The review focuses primarily on TNBC; findings may not be directly generalizable to all solid tumors without considering subtype-specific differences.
    - Many mechanistic claims are based on preclinical models or extrapolated from other cancer types; direct causal evidence in TNBC is limited.
    - Intratumoral microbiome findings are highly susceptible to contamination artifacts; interpretations should be cautious.
    - The review is narrative, not systematic; it does not claim to cover all possible studies or provide a meta-analysis.
##### Topic handles
    - gut microbiota composition and diversity
    - tumor-associated microbiota (mammary/oncobiome)
    - microbial metabolites (SCFAs, indoles, bile acids, TMAO, lactate)
    - immune cell modulation (M1/M2 macrophages, CD8+ T cells, Th17/Treg balance)
    - epigenetic regulation by microbial metabolites
    - methodological challenges in low-biomass samples
    - therapeutic strategies (probiotics, prebiotics, FMT, diet, antibiotics)

### Microbial metabolite-driven immune reprogramming in tumor immunotherapy: mechanisms and therapeutic perspectives

- Source handle: `P0075`
- Paper ID: `1b1b1b6648856c84928d82910c726cd009716f9d`
- DOI: 10.3389/fimmu.2025.1603658

  - **Available material depth:** fulltext

#### Paper understanding from a
    - **Approach:** The authors conduct a narrative synthesis of existing literature, categorizing metabolites by class (SCFAs, Trp derivatives, bile acids, etc.) and mechanism (GPCR signaling, epigenetic modulation, metabolic reprogramming). They analyze context-dependent variables (concentration, tissue specificity, host genetics) to explain functional duality. A translational assessment framework is proposed, weighting criteria such as mechanistic clarity, safety, and clinical evidence to stratify metabolites into priority tiers. The review integrates preclinical mechanistic studies with clinical observational and interventional data to evaluate therapeutic potential.
##### Contribution and limits
    - **1.**
      - **Contribution:** Provides a comprehensive classification of microbial metabolites and their context-dependent mechanisms in tumor immunotherapy.
      - **边界与限制:** Relies heavily on narrative synthesis; causal relationships are often inferred from correlative human data or preclinical models, which may not translate directly to humans.
    - **2.**
      - **Contribution:** Proposes a structured framework for prioritizing metabolites for clinical translation based on multiple weighted criteria.
      - **边界与限制:** The framework's weights and tier assignments are subjective interpretations by the authors; clinical validation of these priorities is ongoing.
    - **3.**
      - **Contribution:** Highlights the dual roles of metabolites (e.g., butyrate, acetate) and emphasizes the importance of concentration and tissue-specific thresholds.
      - **边界与限制:** Many threshold values are derived from animal models or in vitro studies, and precise therapeutic windows in human patients remain to be fully defined.
##### Key findings
    - **1.**
      - **适用条件:** High concentrations (>10 mM) in colonocytes; >100 μM in CRC cells
      - **研究发现:** Butyrate acts as an energy source for normal cells but as an HDAC inhibitor in cancer cells, inducing apoptosis and enhancing CD8+ T cell cytotoxicity via STAT1 acetylation.
    - **2.**
      - **适用条件:** Presence of specific microbial consortia (L. reuteri + C. sporogenes); intratumoral delivery
      - **研究发现:** Indole-3-propionic acid (IPA) sustains CD8+ T cell stemness via H3K27 hyperacetylation at the Tcf7 super-enhancer, synergizing with PD-1 blockade.
    - **3.**
      - **适用条件:** Glucose-deprived TME; presence of IFN-γ
      - **研究发现:** Inosine serves as an alternative carbon source for T cells and drives Th1 differentiation via A2aR signaling, overcoming exhaustion.
    - **4.**
      - **适用条件:** Systemic concentrations >2.5 μM; melanoma context
      - **研究发现:** Butyrate can suppress DC function and promote Treg differentiation, potentially driving resistance to CTLA-4 blockade.
    - **5.**
      - **适用条件:** Elevated kynurenine levels; IDO1 activity
      - **研究发现:** Kynurenine activates AhR to establish a Treg-macrophage suppressive axis, contributing to ICI resistance, though clinical trials of IDO1 inhibitors have failed.
    - **Paper kind:** review
    - **Problem or question:** To address the challenge of variable patient responses to ICIs and the immunosuppressive nature of the TME by elucidating how microbial metabolites mediate host-microbe crosstalk in tumor immunotherapy. It aims to reconcile conflicting data on metabolite effects and establish frameworks for translating these insights into precision therapies.
    - **Research scope:** The review focuses on the role of gut microbiome-derived metabolites (e.g., SCFAs, tryptophan derivatives, bile acids) in modulating tumor immunity and the efficacy of immune checkpoint inhibitors (ICIs) in solid tumors. It covers mechanistic classifications, context-dependent signaling networks, impact on the tumor microenvironment (TME), and clinical translation strategies.
    - **Work summary:** This review synthesizes current evidence on how microbial metabolites reprogram immune cell dynamics and remodel the TME to influence ICI efficacy. It categorizes metabolites by their mechanisms (epigenetic modulation, metabolic reprogramming, receptor signaling) and highlights their dual roles—enhancing or suppressing immunotherapy depending on concentration, tissue context, and host factors. The paper proposes a framework for prioritizing metabolites for clinical translation based on mechanistic clarity, safety, clinical evidence, delivery feasibility, and synergistic potency, while discussing challenges like interindividual variability and causal inference limitations.

It also includes a translational prioritization framework and discusses clinical strategies like probiotics, dietary modulation, and engineered microbes, serving as a rich source for explaining mechanisms and identifying key metabolites for further analysis.

### Salivary microbiome changes distinguish response to chemoradiotherapy in patients with oral cancer

- Source handle: `P0302`
- Paper ID: `83ee66187aff0ae19e2706481c06aa696e21465b`
- DOI: 10.1186/s40168-023-01677-w

  - **Available material depth:** fulltext

#### Paper understanding from a
    - **Approach:** 1. **研究对象与样本收集**：纳入109名SCC患者，收集基线（诊断时）和随访期（6个月或12个月）的刺激性全唾液样本。根据治疗方案分为化疗放疗组（n=50基线，n=33配对）、单纯手术组（n=15配对）等亚组。 2. **微生物组分析**：提取唾液DNA，扩增16S rRNA基因V4区，使用Illumina MiSeq测序。利用mothur软件进行序列处理，基于97%相似度聚类OTUs。使用NMDS、AMOVA评估群落结构差异；使用ALDEx2和LEfSe进行差异丰度分析；使用PICRUSt预测KEGG通路功能。 3. **蛋白表达分析**：对部分患者（n=48用于WB，n=28用于ELISA）的唾液样本进行DMBT1蛋白定量，采用Western Blot和ELISA技术，并使用线性混合模型分析时间效应。 4. **动物实验验证**：在裸鼠皮下注射UM-SCC-1细胞构建肿瘤模型，监测肿瘤生长，并在不同时间点收集唾液，通过Western Blot检测DMBT1表达变化，以验证SCC对宿主蛋白表达的抑制作用。 5. **关联分析**：计算唾液DMBT1蛋白表达水平与微生物组OTU丰度之间的Pearson相关系数，探索两者间的联系。
##### Contribution and limits
    - **1.**
      - **Contribution:** 提供了SCC治疗前后唾液微生物组纵向变化的详细图谱，区分了化疗放疗和单纯手术的不同影响模式。
      - **边界与限制:** 样本量相对有限，特别是单纯手术组和长期随访数据不足，限制了统计效力和对长期预后的推断。
    - **2.**
      - **Contribution:** 首次在同一队列中将唾液微生物组变化与宿主抗菌蛋白DMBT1的表达联系起来，提出了“微生物-宿主蛋白”互作的可能机制线索。
      - **边界与限制:** 相关性分析基于小样本子集（n=20），且为观察性研究，无法确立因果关系；16S测序分辨率限制了对具体菌株功能的精确解析。
    - **3.**
      - **Contribution:** 识别了潜在的预后生物标志物（如基线Prevotella、治疗后Gemella/DMBT1），为无创监测治疗反应提供了方向。
      - **边界与限制:** 发现的特异性细菌标记物需在大独立队列中验证；作者承认多种分析方法（ALDEx2 vs LEfSe）结果不一致，提示方法学敏感性。
##### Key findings
    - **1.**
      - **适用条件:** 所有SCC患者治疗后（6个月 vs 基线）
      - **研究发现:** 唾液微生物组的整体丰富度和多样性显著降低；Firmicutes门丰度增加，Bacteroidetes、Fusobacteria和Proteobacteria门丰度减少；Porphyromonaceae和Prevotellaceae科减少，Lactobacillaceae科增加。
    - **2.**
      - **适用条件:** 化疗放疗组（n=33配对）
      - **研究发现:** 治疗后微生物群落结构发生显著变化；Richness降低但Alpha多样性无显著变化；Prevotellaceae、Pasteurellaceae等减少，Lactobacillaceae增加。
    - **3.**
      - **适用条件:** 单纯手术组（n=15配对）
      - **研究发现:** 治疗后Richness显著降低，但群落结构（Beta多样性）和Alpha多样性无显著变化；LEfSe显示术前富集未分类Lactobacillales spp.，术后富集Pseudomonas。
    - **4.**
      - **适用条件:** 化疗放疗响应者 vs 非响应者（基线）
      - **研究发现:** 群落结构有显著差异，但Alpha多样性无差异；LEfSe显示非响应者基线时Prevotella (OTU0029) 丰度较高，而响应者中未分类Pasteurellaceae、Veillonella等较多。
    - **5.**
      - **适用条件:** 化疗放疗响应者 vs 非响应者（治疗后6个月）
      - **研究发现:** 群落结构差异更显著（p=0.006）；响应者中Veillonella、Streptococcus、Rothia、Gemella等丰度较高；PICRUSt预测响应者氨基酸合成代谢通路富集，非响应者糖代谢和脂肪酸合成通路富集。
    - **6.**
      - **适用条件:** SCC患者唾液DMBT1表达
      - **研究发现:** 治疗前DMBT1表达较低；治疗后6个月和12个月DMBT1表达显著升高（约1.9-2.1倍）。
    - **7.**
      - **适用条件:** 小鼠肿瘤模型（UM-SCC-1注射）
      - **研究发现:** 与对照组相比，携带肿瘤的裸鼠唾液DMBT1分泌显著减少，证实SCC抑制宿主DMBT1表达。
    - **8.**
      - **适用条件:** DMBT1表达与微生物组相关性
      - **研究发现:** 治疗后DMBT1水平的增加与Gemella、Pasteurellaceae、Lactobacillus和Oribacterium等菌群丰度的增加呈正相关。
    - **Paper kind:** empirical
    - **Problem or question:** 1. SCC治疗（特别是化疗放疗与手术）如何改变患者的唾液微生物组结构和功能？ 2. 治疗前后的唾液微生物组特征是否能预测SCC患者对化疗放疗的反应？ 3. 唾液中的抗菌蛋白DMBT1在治疗过程中的表达变化如何，且其与微生物组的变化是否存在关联？
    - **Research scope:** 口腔鳞状细胞癌（SCC）患者的唾液微生物组及抗菌蛋白DMBT1表达；涉及化疗放疗联合治疗与单纯手术治疗的纵向对比，以及治疗反应（响应者vs非响应者）的关联分析。
    - **Work summary:** 本研究是一项针对口腔鳞状细胞癌（SCC）患者的纵向研究，旨在探讨癌症治疗对唾液微生物组的影响，并考察这些变化与治疗反应及唾液抗菌蛋白DMBT1表达之间的关联。研究纳入了接受不同治疗方案（化疗放疗、单纯手术等）的患者，通过16S rRNA基因测序分析基线（0个月）和治疗后（6个月）的唾液样本。结果显示，治疗后唾液微生物组的丰富度和多样性普遍降低，且特定菌群结构发生显著变化。在化疗放疗患者中，响应者与未响应者在治疗前后的微生物组成存在差异，其中Prevotella属与非响应相关，而Gemella属等与响应相关。此外，研究发现SCC会抑制唾液DMBT1的表达，治疗后DMBT1水平回升，且DMBT1水平的增加与Gemella等特定菌群的丰度增加呈正相关。该研究首次在同一队列中纵向追踪了SCC治疗引起的唾液微生物组变化及其与宿主免疫蛋白表达的关联。

    - **1.**

      - **Material:** SCC患者治疗后唾液微生物组多样性降低、特定菌群（Lactobacillaceae增加，Porphyromonaceae减少）变化的描述。

      - **Material:** DMBT1在肿瘤状态下被抑制，治疗后恢复并与有益菌群（Gemella）增加相关的发现。
